- **A terminal session ends with its shell, on Linux too.** When the shell exits, the session ends as soon as what is already in the terminal has been read, a quarter of a second of quiet: a background job the shell left running is not kept, and what it writes after that is not in the session. Before, a Linux session lasted while any job still held the terminal, writing or silent, until every one let go or the session was killed.
- **The terminal host runs on a person's own machine, with no server.** `sail _pty-host --local` is the same host, serving the account it runs as on `~/.sail/local/pty.sock` in the home the account database names. Before anything is bound, each directory down to it is read without following a link, as ssh's StrictModes reads `~/.ssh` and its parents: the home and `~/.sail` must be the person's (the home may be root's) and writable by no one else, though others may read them, so the 755 `~/.sail` other commands make serves; `~/.sail/local`, made owner-only when absent, must be the person's and open to no one else. Anything else is refused at start, naming the path, its owner and mode and the fix ("Run 'chmod go-w ~/.sail'." for one others may write; "Remove it, and the host makes the directory itself." for `~/.sail/local`, a link or a file). What the host keeps there (`sessions`, `pty.lock`, `pty.sock`) must be of its own kind and the person's, not a link, or the start names it and says to remove it. A connection that presents no token is that person; any token is refused ("This terminal host serves this machine's own person alone, who presents no token, so it takes none: connect again presenting none."). An open that names a project or a room is refused ("The local terminal host opens folders, not projects or rooms: open the session with neither, in the folder it runs in."). A session runs in the folder its open names, an existing directory named by its absolute path that the person may enter, and anything else is refused before a process starts. With no command it runs the account's login shell from the account database (`getpwuid`, not `$SHELL`) as a login shell; a login shell that is missing or cannot be run is refused at start, naming `chsh`. Its environment adds `HOME`, `USER`, `LOGNAME` and `SHELL` from that entry, `TERM`, and a UTF-8 character type: nothing more when the first of `LC_ALL`, `LC_CTYPE` and `LANG` the host has names a UTF-8 locale and the system has, else `C.UTF-8` where it has that (each asked of the C library), else `en_US.UTF-8`, as `LANG` and as each of the others the host sets. The local host opens no database, runs no `incus` and binds no port, and a ticket request hears that tickets come from a server's terminal host. Its sessions outlive their clients and end when it stops, which removes their transcripts and sidecars, and a new host has a new boot id. A second host for the same person exits saying where the first serves, in another process or the same one; a host whose predecessor died replaces its socket, and one started while its predecessor is still stopping is refused. A home directory whose socket path would pass 102 bytes (macOS's 104-byte `sun_path`, less the two the JDK keeps) is refused at start, naming the path and the limit. `_pty-host --local`, `_pty-child` and `_pty-selftest` run on the machine: in client mode instead of being sent to the server, and for an account that does not own a Linux box with root's `sail-api`, which is no longer refused them. A local command in client mode prints its failure as a message instead of a stack trace. `sail _pty-selftest` now starts a local host, opens a session in a folder, has a line evaluated, detaches, attaches again and stops, printing `local-selftest: ok`, which the native smoke tests and the release require.
- **Ending a terminal session hangs it up first, on every box.** A kill, a pty host's stop for good, a dispatch that displaces a session from the repos it reserves, the sweep reaping a session never attached, and the local host's stop now send the session's shim `SIGHUP`, as a closing terminal does, which the shim passes to the command. A session still running a second later is sent `SIGTERM`, and one still running 5 s after that is killed with everything under it, as before. An interactive shell, which ignores `SIGTERM`, now ends at once and writes its history, where it was killed after 5 s; its background jobs (`npm run dev &`) now end with it, since the shell passes the hangup on, where they outlived it; a `nohup`'d job survives as before. A command that handled only `SIGTERM` hears `SIGHUP` first and, unless it handles that too, ends on it; one that ignores the hangup is sent `SIGTERM` a second later, so ending it takes that second longer than it did.
- **A pty host that cannot make its socket's directory owner-only does not start.** The failed `chmod` was ignored, and the host listened anyway.

## 0.66.0

- **The box credential in a project container has a lane of its own, with no human gate.** Whatever runs in a container can read `box.credential`, so the socket now resolves it on the `container` lane (`whoami` shows `lane: container`, was `cli`), whose column allows reading, posting, creating drafts, changing what its person owns, claiming and serving, and nothing else. Refused, each `403 agent_lane_forbidden` with a fix naming the door outside: reassigning a spec, "The credential in a project container cannot assign specs." with "Do it from outside the container: 'sail spec update e1 --assignee <fde>' on the host, or Mast."; every move of a spec's status and creating a spec in any status but draft, "The credential in a project container cannot approve specs." with "Do it from outside the container: 'sail spec update e1 --status <status>' on the host, or Mast.", naming the spec; sharing or removing a skill or a project file, "The credential in a project container cannot share files and skills in projects." with "Do it from outside the container: 'sail project skills add --container <folder> -p acme' or 'sail project skills rm <skill> -p acme' on the host, or Mast.". The role is still asked first, so a member's reassignment stays admin-only. This is narrower than the design's "promote and mark done": archiving and cancelling are status moves too, and are refused. A person's own terminal, IDE or interactive agent in the container loses the same gates: `spec update <id> --status ...` and `spec create --status pending` there are refused, including an instruction file that tells an interactive agent to run them; move a status on the board in Mast or with `sail spec update` on the host. A person's credential in the container still reads every message of a conversation.
- **The socket serves no sync health.** `GET /v1/sync` on the container socket answered the box's person; it is gone for every caller: `404` "No route for /v1/sync" (a `POST` there answered `405`, and is `404` too). `sail sync` on the host and the web API keep it.
- **A move of a spec's status, and a spec born ready, take Approve.** For a person nothing changes: whoever may make the edit, claim the spec or create it may move or set its status, and hears what they heard before. An agent may not: setting any status, or creating a spec with `--status pending`, is `403 agent_lane_forbidden` "An agent creates only drafts and never sets a status." with "Omit --status; its person promotes it on the board.".
- **An agent acts only on its own work.** A build, review or fix agent works its spec, in that spec's room; a room member its room and, in a spec's room, that spec; an ad-hoc run its whole project; and every agent the specs it made, read from the journal's first revision of each. Posting outside it is `403 forbidden_not_assignee` "'claude/<run>' may post only in its own work, not in 'den'."; editing, deleting or restoring a spec outside it "'claude/<run>' may change only its own work, not spec 's-bob'."; creating a spec in another project, or born in another room, "'claude/<run>' may create only its own work, not spec 'x'."; moving a spec it made, or the spec it works, into another project (`--project` on update), "'claude/<run>' may change only its own work, not spec 'd1'.", and a spec or room filed under another project is no agent's work, whoever made it, nor is the room a spec left behind when a person moved it there ("'claude/<run>' may post only in its own work, not in 'd1'."); each with "An agent acts only on the work sail launched it for; draft a follow-up spec for anything else.". Inside its work the owner is still asked, as before. A spec an agent made stays its own work while this box's history keeps the spec's first revision; once compaction drops it (after 20 newer revisions), it is the agent's only if it is the spec its run works. An agent creates no room but the own room of a spec it creates, and starts or stops only its own project's processes. A read-only room agent posting outside its room heard `403` "A room session posts only to its own room." with no fix; it now hears the words above.
- **An agent claims nothing, resolves no conflict, and reads only its own project's definition.** Claiming a spec, even for its person, is `403 agent_lane_forbidden` "Agent principals cannot claim specs." with "Only an operator lane (CLI or API token) may do this.". Reading the definition of a project other than its own, which it read as its person, is now `403 forbidden_not_assignee` "'claude/<run>' may read only its own work, not project 'beta'.". Resolving a parked conflict, any strategy, is `403 forbidden_not_assignee` "'claude/<run>' resolves no conflicts: an agent acts only on its own work." with "Ask the FDE the agent works for to resolve it.".
- **A read-only room agent reads its room's project alone.** It read every project its person reads; a spec, room or run of another project is now not found, and lists leave them out.
- **An agent reads the skills and ports of its person's other projects.** A run's `/v1/projects/{project}/skills` and `/ports` were pinned to its own project ("Project 'zeta' is not in the catalog."); a run now reads every project its person reads, as the permission table's Read says. Serving stays its own project's.
- **An agent's announcement is not taken at its word, and an agent's event about work outside its projects is not found.** An agent publishing `spec_message_posted` heard "Event type 'spec_message_posted' is not available to agent principals."; it now hears the event rule's own refusal, `403 forbidden_not_assignee`, since the socket strips `source` and an agent retells nothing. An agent's event about work outside its scope is now not found (`404`, in the words an event about work this box does not hold hears), where it heard the agent sentence. Its own narration, and every other refusal, keep their words.
- **Skills are shared from outside the container.** Nothing in a container may share or remove a skill: `skill add` and `skill rm` there are refused before the folder is read, the box credential with the lane's words above, a run with "Agent principals cannot share files and skills in projects." and a room run as read-only, where a run heard "A skill is shared by a person, so an agent sail launched may not share one." and the box credential could. A person shares a folder that lives in the container with `sail project skills add --container /home/dev/<folder> -p <project>` on the host, or, for Mast, `POST /v1/projects/{project}/skills` with `{"folder": ..., "name": ...}`, which reads a container on that box and so is its own person's or an admin's (anyone else, a member of the project too, hears `403 forbidden_not_assignee` "Box 'uday' belongs to 'uday', not you." with "Ask uday to operate it, or have an admin do it."), and removes one with `DELETE /v1/projects/{project}/skills/{name}`, Share alone; each is decided before anything is read. The socket serves neither: in the container, `skill add` and `skill rm` hear the refusal and nothing is read. A machine token still creates a ready spec, as before. A skill made by hand in an agent's folder lists `sail project skills add --container /home/dev/.claude/skills/<name> -p <project>` as its `share` in `sail project skills ls --json` on the host (was `skill add ~/.claude/skills/<name>`), and `sail project skills ls` says "share it with 'sail project skills add --container … -p acme'"; in the container, `skill ls` names no `share` (`null`), since nothing there may run one. The in-container `skill` help lists `ls` and `show` and names the host commands.
- **The in-container help and the spec-board skill teach no status move.** `spec --help` drops `--status` from `create` and `update`, and `spec archive`, a status move too (typed anyway, it is refused as one), and says a spec created there is a draft and its status moves on the board in Mast or with `sail spec update <id> --status <status>` on the host; the installed `spec-board` skill says the same, and that an agent claims nothing.
- **The session report finds its credential as every helper does.** `sail-session-report` now falls back to `box.credential` whenever `SAIL_RUN_CREDENTIAL` is unset, as `spec`, `skill` and the portal do, where it did only without `SAIL_RUN_ID`. A run's report on the box credential is refused by the socket, and the hook still exits 0 silently.
- **Upgrading.** Nothing is migrated: the box credential takes the `container` lane, and every live run its work, when sail-api restarts on this release, so a run under way is narrowed at once. A node still on 0.65 decides its own socket by the old rules until it upgrades.
- **A large sync frame is read in time linear in its size.** A sync line, a blob manifest or a live-stream line was parsed in time that grew with the square of its longest string: a 4 MiB message body took seconds to read, and a frame near the 16 MiB ceiling minutes. Each now reads in tens to a few hundred milliseconds.

## 0.65.0

- **Sync health, bus stats and sync rounds are the box's own person's, or an admin's.** `GET /v1/sync`, `POST /v1/sync` and `GET /v1/events/stats` answered any authenticated caller. Another FDE now gets `403 forbidden_not_assignee` "Box 'uday' belongs to 'uday', not you." with "Ask uday to operate it, or have an admin do it.". On a box that names no FDE of its own (no sync handle, as a standalone box has by default), anyone but an admin now gets `403 forbidden_not_assignee` "This box has no owner, so only an admin may operate it.". A machine token acts for no one, with the role of the box's person capped by the role it was minted with, so one that is not an admin there is refused the same way: one minted below admin, and one minted admin on a box whose person is not an admin. On the container socket, a run or a room agent reading `GET /v1/sync` gets `403 agent_lane_forbidden` "Agent principals cannot operate boxes."; the box credential still reads it.
- **The box's own person may point it at another main.** `POST /v1/sync` with `{"main": "..."}` was admin-only (`403 forbidden`); the box's own person may now do it too, as anyone who may run its rounds.
- **A viewer publishes their own box's events.** A viewer whose box it is may now publish `board_updated` and `snapshot_created` for it, where they were refused as read-only; another viewer is refused `forbidden_not_assignee`, "Event type 'board_updated' speaks for this box, and you may not drive what it does: only this box's FDE or an admin publishes it.", with "Ask this box's FDE or an admin to publish it.".
- **A run's narration is believed only on the box that ran it, an admin's included.** An admin publishing `agent_session_started` or another of a run's reports about a run another box executed was believed; it is now refused `forbidden_not_assignee`, as anyone else's. Whoever sends it hears "Event type 'agent_session_started' reports what another box observed of run … : only the box that executed it reports that.", with "Publish it from bob, which executed it; what it reports reaches this box by sync." when this box knows which box ran it, and "What another box did reaches this one by sync." when it does not, where everyone heard that only this box's FDE or an admin reports it.
- **Reading a run's log is operating the run.** `GET /v1/runs/{id}/log`, the log stream and `sail agent log` now ask what a stop asks, before where the run executed: a viewer, the run's own viewer included, is refused "Your credential is read-only and cannot stop or attach to runs or read their logs." with "Ask an admin for a member or admin credential.", an agent "Agent principals cannot stop or attach to runs or read their logs.", and another member is told whose run it is, where they were told the run executed on another box. The refusal of a stop now reads "cannot stop or attach to runs or read their logs." too, since operating a run is stopping it, attaching to it and reading its log.
- **A preview stop is decided as the stop.** `sail agent stop --dry-run` and a project-wide stop were not asked whether the caller may stop the run; both are now refused as the stop would be, before the container is asked, and a project-wide stop of a project outside your projects answers "Project 'acme' is not in the catalog.", where it named the run or stopped nothing.
- **Dispatch decides before it asks whether the container runs.** A dispatch that is not yours, or that runs on another box, was answered "Project 'acme' is stopped." while its container was down; it now hears whose it is, or where it runs, whatever the container's state.
- **The host CLI reads the catalog as its person.** On a box whose FDE main's roster scopes, every host command that reads the catalog now reads it as that FDE, so a project outside its grants is one the catalog does not hold: `sail project list` leaves it out, `sail project apply --all` neither converges nor names its container, and `sail agent status`, `sail agent report`, `sail agent log`, `sail agent attach`, `sail project config show`, `sail project config get` and a project-wide `sail agent stop` answer "Project 'beta' is not in the catalog.", while the project mutations (`config set`, `repo add`, `service add`, `service remove`, `resources set`) answer "No project 'beta' in the catalog.", as for a project the box does not hold. A project the box does not hold is told the same way: `sail project config show` and `config get` said "No project 'beta' in the catalog. Create it with …", and `sail agent status`, `sail agent attach` and `sail agent log` asked its container first; each now says "Project 'beta' is not in the catalog." with "Create it with 'sail project create beta', or sync it from main with 'sail sync'.". `sail agent attach` asks what a stop asks of the run it attaches to, so a viewer is refused it, "Your credential is read-only and cannot stop or attach to runs or read their logs." A preview acts as the box's person too and is refused as the command it rehearses: `--dry-run` of a stop or a project mutation, and `--dry-run` or `--json` of `sail agent run` and `sail agent sweep`. Five commands still read as the box itself: the server, `sail sync`, `sail sync status`, `sail migrate` and `sail upgrade`. The container verbs (`sail project start`, `stop`, `switch`, `containers`, `logs`, `exec`, `shell`, `connect` and `sail project snapshot …`) never consult the catalog: they act on this box's containers by name with its own privileges, as before.
- **The SSH gateway runs only the `agent` verbs that ask the API.** Over an SSH-key session, `sail agent stream`, `sessions` and `review` run as the caller's FDE, as before. The other `agent` verbs (`stop`, `logs`, `status`, `attach`, `report`, `sweep`, `run`, `watch`) read the box's control plane directly as its own person, whoever called; the gateway now refuses them: "'agent stop' reads this box's control plane directly, as the box's own person, so it is not available over an SSH-key session. Follow the work with 'sail spec' and the board, or SSH to the host directly to run it.".
- **A node that has not placed its person refuses to guess.** A node whose roster does not know its FDE yet, between `sail join` and the first sync after main authorises its key, now refuses a command that reads projects, specs or runs, where it answered as the box itself: "This box does not know its FDE's role ('mady'), so it cannot tell what you may do." with "Run 'sudo sail sync' first.". sail-api syncs a node once a minute, so a node is placed within a minute of main authorising it; `sudo sail sync` places it at once.
- **A machine token and a run acting for no one see the box's person's projects.** A token that names no FDE, and a run that acts for no FDE, saw every project; each now sees what the box's FDE sees.
- **A box credential minted for another FDE is refused.** The socket's box credential stands for the box's person when it is used; one minted while the box named another FDE now gets `401` until it is minted again.
- **A room post with no FDE behind it is the message rule's refusal.** A machine token, or the host token on a box with no sync handle, posting in a room got `403 forbidden` "Room messages require an FDE-bound credential so authorship can be synchronized."; it now hears the message rule, in its order: a room this box does not hold is `404 room_not_found` "Room 'ghost-room' was not found.", a viewer's token is `403 read_only_credential` "Your credential is read-only and cannot post in rooms.", and any other, an admin's included, is `403 forbidden_not_author` "This credential acts for no FDE, so a post in 'lobby' would be no one's." with "Post with an FDE's own credential.". A message a node pushes with no author is refused by the same rule, where main read it as malformed.
- **Main serves a sync session only when an FDE stands behind it.** A `_sync` session with no token, a token that is no live session, or one whose FDE is gone was served as an anonymous reader; main now refuses it before anything is served, and so a disabled FDE's session, with one line: "sync: main serves no session whose token is missing, invalid or expired, or whose FDE is disabled or removed. Ask an admin to re-enable the FDE, then sync again.", where a disabled FDE heard "sync: this session's FDE is disabled on main. Ask an admin to re-enable it.".
- **Managing people is admin-only first, at the SSH gateway and on a node.** At the gateway a member's `sail fde ...` said "'fde' requires the admin role."; it now says "Only an admin may manage people. Ask an admin to do it.", and a viewer's "Your credential is read-only and cannot manage people. Ask an admin for a member or admin credential.". On a node, `sail fde add`, `update`, `grant`, `revoke` and `rm` by a member answer "Only an admin may manage people." with "Ask an admin to do it." before saying the roster is managed on main, and a node that cannot place its own FDE says that first.
- **A disabled person enrolls, registers and signs in to nothing.** A ticket minted before the disable no longer starts an enrollment, a registration started before it no longer finishes, by ticket or by an admin, and a passkey of a disabled person mints no session and is not counted as used: each answers `401 authentication_failed` "FDE 'obs' is disabled. Ask an admin to re-enable it.". An admin minting a ticket for a disabled person, or starting or finishing a registration for them, is told `409 conflict` "FDE 'obs' is disabled.".
- **An enrollment ticket finishes only its own person's registration.** A ticket presented with another FDE's registration challenge is refused `422 invalid_request` "Registration challenge is not for the FDE this enrollment ticket enrolls. Start the registration again with this ticket.", and nothing is saved.
- **An admin's passkey session mints enrollment tickets.** `POST /v1/auth/register/ticket` took only an admin API token; an admin signed in with a passkey may now mint one, and start a registration without a ticket. At both, a member is refused `403 forbidden_admin_only` "Only an admin may manage people." with "Ask an admin to do it.", and a viewer `403 read_only_credential` "Your credential is read-only and cannot manage people.". With an API token both heard "Your role is not permitted to perform this action."; with a passkey session both heard `403 invalid_bearer_token` "Bearer token is invalid.", since the passkey routes took no session. A session that is invalid, expired or a disabled person's is now told at the passkey routes, as at every other route, `403 invalid_bearer_token` "Session token is invalid or expired.", where it heard "Bearer token is invalid.".
- **The event stream answers its own path, and asks the caller first.** `/v1/events/stream` served any path that only began with it: `/v1/events/stream/x` and `/v1/events/streamx` were streamed to an authenticated caller; each now answers `404 not_found` "API endpoint was not found.". The stream reads its path as every API route does, so a trailing or doubled slash (`/v1/events/stream/`, `/v1/events/stream//`) still streams. A method other than GET was refused `405` before any credential was asked; the caller is now asked first, as at every API route, so with no credential it hears `401 missing_bearer_token`, with an invalid, expired or revoked one, or one whose person is disabled, `403 invalid_bearer_token`, and with a valid one `405` on the stream's own path and `404` on any other.
- **A terminal opens only in a project its person holds on the box.** Opening or reattaching a session in a project the catalog does not hold, or that your grants leave out, now answers "Project 'beta' is not in the catalog.", whatever your role: a viewer still opens one in their own projects. A terminal with no session token acts as the box's person on the CLI lane, as the host CLI does.
- **A terminal the host cannot check opens nothing, and stays connected.** Opening or reattaching a session while the control plane cannot answer whether its project is yours closed the connection with "The host could not serve that request."; the host now answers "The host could not serve that request. Nothing was opened or changed; try again, and if it keeps failing, check the pty host's log." and keeps the connection, and says the same wherever else it cannot serve a request.
- **An open terminal follows a change of access within one sweep.** The terminal host severed a connection only once its credential stopped resolving; it now severs one whose person's role or projects changed, at the next sweep, and nothing while the database cannot answer.

## 0.64.0

- **One rule decides who may do what, so one person hears one reason at every door.** Who may do what is unchanged: everything allowed before is allowed, and everything refused is refused. What may change is the reason a refused person is told, which now comes in one order wherever they ask: what lies outside their projects is not found first, then what this box does not hold (a project, or a conversation a spec is born or a message posted in), then a read-only or admin-only credential, then an agent's credential, then whose the work is, then the work's own facts, and last which box runs it. So a viewer who stops a run another box executed hears "Your credential is read-only and cannot stop runs.", where they heard that the run executed elsewhere; a viewer who moves a spec into a project this box does not hold, creates one born in a conversation it does not hold, or posts in one, hears that the project or the conversation is not on this box, where they heard read-only; resolving a conflict that is not open answers "No open conflict for 'x'." before a read-only credential is told it cannot resolve conflicts; pruning a spec in a project you are not granted answers "Spec 'x' was not found." before it says read-only; dispatching someone else's spec answers `forbidden_not_assignee` "Spec 's-ada' is assigned to 'ada', not you." with "Ask ada to dispatch it, or have an admin reassign it before dispatching.", where it answered `not_your_spec`; and an event published about work outside your projects is a 404 naming work this box does not hold, where it was refused as read-only or not yours. Main answers a node in the same order: a request to erase what lies outside the pusher's projects is told main holds none of it before it is told read-only, and a run or review pushed on someone else's spec names that spec's owner before anything else is checked.
- **A read-only credential is told what it tried to do.** A viewer heard "Your credential is read-only and cannot change specs." (or files, or projects) whatever they tried. The refusal now names the action: "cannot create specs.", "cannot assign specs.", "cannot claim specs.", "cannot post in rooms." (at the terminal's room pin too), "cannot approve reviews.", "cannot configure projects.", "cannot share files and skills in projects.", "cannot restore or delete snapshots.", "cannot start or stop a project's processes.", "cannot manage room membership.", "cannot prune specs.", "cannot purge projects.". An agent's credential refused room membership hears "Agent principals cannot manage room membership.", where it heard "Agent credentials cannot manage room membership."; an agent's prune hears "Agent principals cannot prune specs." (by id) or "Pruning by policy or a whole project is admin-only." (by policy), where it heard "An agent cannot prune: erasing is irreversible and belongs to the FDE.".
- **Claiming a spec born in a room names the room's owners.** Claiming a spec born in someone else's room is still refused as admin-only (`forbidden_admin_only`), and now names who may start work there: "'design' belongs to uday: only uday or an admin may start work there, not you.", with "Ask an admin to assign it to you.".
- **A project this box does not hold says how to get it.** Creating, moving or pushing a spec or room into a project the box's catalog does not hold, on a box whose FDE every project is granted to, answered "Project 'omega' isn't on this box. Projects here: …" and named nothing to do. It now adds "Create it with 'sail project create omega', or sync it from main with 'sail sync'.", as a read of a project the catalog does not hold already did. A request to main to erase a project it does not hold says "main holds no project 'x'" before it says the credential is read-only or the erase is admin-only.
- **A run or review that names nothing to own says so.** A run that names no spec and acts for no one was refused as "Run x is an ad-hoc session." with "Only its owner or an admin may access this run."; it now says "Run x names no spec and no one it acts for." with "Only an admin may access this run.". A review that names no spec said "Review x is for spec 'null', which has no owner."; it now says "Review x names no spec, so it has no owner." with "Only an admin may approve or dismiss it.".
- **A run that is not there is said one way.** A run's inbox, messages and the other run routes answered "Run 'x' was not found." where stop and the log said "No run 'x'."; every route now says "No run 'x'.".
- **Error codes are spelled the same under every locale.** A refusal's code (`read_only_credential`, `forbidden_not_assignee`, …) was lower-cased in the server's default locale, so on a Turkish locale a code with an `I` came out dotless (`forbıdden`). It is now lower-cased in the root locale.
- **Every refused event says whom to ask.** An event refused because it speaks for the box now says "Ask this box's FDE or an admin to publish it.", one an agent's credential may not publish "Ask the FDE the agent works for, or an admin, to publish it.", and an agent narrating another run "An agent acts only on the work sail launched it for.", where each named no fix.
- **A member's rename says what makes it admin-only.** Renaming a project erases its old name everywhere, as purging it does, so a member's rename, at the CLI or pushed from a node, now answers "Only an admin may erase the name of project 'acme' everywhere, as purging or renaming it does." with "Ask an admin to do it.", where it said the rename moves every spec and file in the project. A prune by policy, or of a whole project, still answers "Pruning by policy or a whole project is admin-only." with the prune a member may make.
- **An event stream's heartbeat counts from the moment the stream opens.** A stream read the time its heartbeat counts from only after it said it had subscribed, so a stream's first heartbeat, and the check of its caller's access that rides on it, could come later than a second after it opened. It now counts from before it says so. Found by CI, as a test that waited for a disabled FDE's idle stream to end.

## 0.63.0

- **A definition that leaves out the agent's flags means what `project create` writes.** The GCP fleet set a project's `agent` section as the README shows it, `{"type": "claude-code", "install": [...]}`, and dispatch made no work branch: the agent chose its own and opened a pull request sail never learned of. A definition read an absent `auto_branch` or `auto_snapshot` as false and an absent `branch_prefix` as none, where `sail project create` writes `true`, `agent/` and `true`. An absent flag now means those values, and an explicit one, `false` included, is kept. A stored definition is not rewritten: a project whose `agent` section names none of the flags begins branching under `agent/` and snapshotting before a run at this upgrade. Set `"auto_branch": false` (or `"auto_snapshot": false`) to keep the old behavior. A project that turns branching on but names no prefix branches under `agent/`, where it used `sail/`.
- **sail's own YAML files are replaced whole.** `host.yaml`, the client and server connection configs and sail's other YAML files were rewritten in place, so a reader that opened one mid-write, such as sail-api asking whether the box is still a node, could read it empty or cut short. Every such write now goes to a file beside it and is moved over it in one step, keeping its permissions, so a reader sees the old file or the new one.
- **A build that ends on its own question waits for the answer.** The GCP fleet's agent loop found a build that asked which of three fixes to make, with nothing committed yet, and ended its turn. Twenty-eight seconds later the loop moved the spec to `review`: the reviewer found nothing to review, errored three times and escalated with "fix the reviewer, then re-dispatch with --restart", telling the person who should have answered the question to fix the reviewer. Now a build or fix agent whose newest question in the spec's room is one no owner or admin has answered (exactly when the board says it needs a reply, whatever the agent said after it) leaves the spec `in_progress`, unreviewed, and the room says so once, naming the question. The owner's or an admin's reply, from any door, synced or local, resumes it on the box of the spec's assignee, with the question and the answer in its prompt: a build goes on as a build, on its branch checked out over the tree it left (nothing claimed, snapshotted or reset, and no restart recorded), a fix agent goes on with the same findings. An answer the run heard before it ended holds nothing; one that came after it stopped hearing the room resumes it at its stop. Another member's reply, which never reaches the agent, resumes nothing, and a resume that cannot start says so in the room. A review that errors three times in a row now names the last error it saw, where it said "fix the reviewer" whatever had failed.
- **A room's live member is gone from its room once its run is told stopped.** When a live member's process ended, sail announced its run stopped and only then let go of it, so a room stream opened in between (as Mast's is, on hearing the stop) was told the dead member's last turn, such as a failed `error_max_turns`, rather than that the room has no member. A member now stops answering for its room the moment it begins to leave. Found by CI, as a test that read the room right after the stop.

## 0.62.0

- **Every FDE who may write in a project speaks in every conversation of it.** A post in a spec's or room's conversation was admitted only from its owner (the assignee, or the creator while unassigned) or an admin, so the creator of a spec could no longer comment once someone else held it ("'shared' belongs to eng: only eng or an admin may post there, not you.", found by the GCP fleet). A writer (member or admin) who acts for an FDE now posts in any conversation of the projects their scope admits, at the HTTP API, the host CLI, the container's `spec comment`, the terminal's room pin and at main's decision on a synced message alike; a viewer is still refused as read-only everywhere. A room's own agent (the room lane) still posts only for an owner of its conversation, and a room's settings stay its owner's. A member's post in a conversation this box does not hold, deleted or never synced here, is refused as `room_not_found` ("No conversation 'gone' is on this box: it was deleted, or never here."), where the ownership rule refused it before. Starting work in a room, a spec born there or a claim of one born there, stays with the room's owners and an admin (`SeatingRule`), as before: work seated in a room makes its owner one of that room's owners, whose room agent speaks there and who drives its events, so speaking in a room gives no share of it. A credential that acts for no FDE cannot post: its post would be no one's. A message's announcement follows the same rule. Speaking is not steering: only a conversation's owners and an admin reach the agents at work in it (`RoomStore.steering`), since an agent runs on its owner's box with their credentials. Another member's comment is read by people, and never by that agent: not in a running build's inbox, not as a wake, not in the room the build, review, fix or room agent is shown, and not when the agent reads the room itself with `spec comments`. It does not answer the agent's question either, so the question keeps paging the owner. A run's own words count as those of the FDE it acts for, so the owner's reviewer still reaches the fix turn, and a running build still hears its own FDE after the spec is reassigned. A spec born in a room is now dispatched with that room's conversation, where the prompt read an empty one. `sail` is no longer a valid FDE handle, since sail's own words steer every agent. For the same reason, main accepts sail's narration from a node only while that node's FDE steers the conversation, so a box whose spec was reassigned no longer narrates in its room.
- **A review whose finding quotes a fenced code block is read whole.** The GCP fleet's agent loop found every review of a README change erroring three times and escalating ("reviewer output unparseable: Failed to parse JSON: while scanning a quoted scalar"): the reviewer's finding quoted the fix it wanted, a markdown section with a ```` ```bash ```` block, and the verdict's block ended at that inner fence, so the JSON was cut short. A verdict's block now ends only at a fence that opens its own line, which a JSON string, holding no line break, never does, and the next block is looked for after that fence, so a fence quoted inside a finding opens no block of its own either.
- **Dispatching a draft says how to make it ready.** `sail spec dispatch --spec <id>` of a draft answered "To dispatch it again, run … --restart (this resets its status to pending and records the restart as a lifecycle event)", for a spec never dispatched, whose restart would be recorded though none happened. It now says "Spec '<id>' is a draft. A spec is dispatched only when pending. Make it ready with sail spec update <id> --status pending, then dispatch it." Every other status keeps the `--restart` remedy.

## 0.61.0

- **A project this box does not hold is refused, never answered as empty.** The GCP fleet found that on a node granted only `acme`, `sail project files ls -p beta` printed "No shared files on beta." and `sail spec list -p beta` "No specs found for project 'beta'", both exiting 0, though beta has files and specs; a misspelt name read the same way, and `sail project files add` wrote into a project the catalog did not hold. Every read and act that names a project, at the CLI and over the API, now refuses one this box's catalog does not hold with "Project 'beta' is not in the catalog.", exactly as one outside the caller's scope is refused, so a node cannot tell a project it was not granted from one that does not exist.
- **A viewer can apply a project that names an agent.** The GCP fleet found that `sudo sail project apply beta` on a viewer's node provisioned the whole container and then failed on its last step with "Your credential is read-only and cannot create rooms.": apply makes the box's FDE a home room with the project's agent seated, and a viewer may create no room. A viewer posts in no room, so it now has no home room: its apply says "No home room: this box's FDE holds a read-only credential and posts in no room, so it has no home room." and succeeds, and `GET /v1/projects/{p}` names no `home_room` for a viewer, so Mast never opens one that cannot exist.

## 0.60.0

- **A node syncs its writes as it makes them.** The GCP fleet found that a node joined by the README never synced on its own: `sail spec create` on a node reached main only at the next `sudo sail sync`, and an idle node never pulled. sail-api decided once, when it started, whether the box was a node, and `sudo sail init --main` starts sail-api before the join makes it one. sail-api now asks on every round: a write on a node reaches main within the debounce (2 s) and an idle node pulls every minute, from the moment it joins, with no restart. A box that stops being a node stops syncing the same way. When the box's role cannot be read, the round is skipped and logged once, and its recovery is logged once. Slack narration follows the same live answer, so a box narrates exactly while it is not a node.
- **The in-container `spec`, `skill` and `portal` CLIs answer in sail's words alone.** A refusal printed curl's own `curl: (22) The requested URL returned error: 403` above sail's JSON. The CLIs now print sail's refusal and exit 1, and an API that does not answer at all is named: "spec: the sail API at /var/lib/sail/run/api.sock did not answer (curl exit 7)". Containers take the new CLIs at their next `sudo sail project apply` or `sudo sail migrate`.
- **A terminal shows what its program said as it was ended.** Closing a session ended the pty as soon as its child exited, so the last lines a program wrote while it was being stopped (a TERM trap's farewell) could be lost. The pty now drains what the child wrote before it ends. An SSE stream whose subscription is closed ends at once, instead of at its next event.
- **Every release is proven on a fleet.** `ci/fleet` stands up a main and three FDE nodes on GCP (an admin, a member of every project, a member scoped to one, a viewer scoped to two) from the README's commands. It checks the roster, each node's projects and that nothing else is in its database, each role's writes at the node CLI, the in-container `spec` CLI and the HTTP API Mast calls, a conflict parked and resolved the same everywhere, and an upgrade from the previous release. CI takes about 6 minutes, down from 13 to 18, and a release builds only after the tagged commit's CI on main has passed.

## 0.59.0

- **A project is its definition in the catalog, and nothing else.** A definition was a `sail.yaml` on disk and a copy of it in the catalog, kept in step by importers, a materializer, placeholders and a file fallback, and its identity fields were rewritten in and out on every sync. It is now one typed, sectioned record in the catalog (`ProjectDefinition`: description, image, resources, runtimes, repos, services, processes, packages, agent, pipeline), read and written as JSON through one codec, and checked whole wherever it is written: a value of the wrong type, or a key no section has, is refused naming the field and that section's keys. `sail project init` is `sail project create <name>` (flags for every section, the questions when none are given, `--definition -` for a whole definition on stdin), and refuses a name already in the catalog; `sail project edit` opens the definition, or one `--section`, as JSON in `$EDITOR` and writes each section that changed; `sail project config show|get|set` reads and writes one section from scripts. Each section is written over the version it was read from, so an edit made meanwhile on another box is refused, never overwritten. Mast reads and writes the same over `GET /v1/projects/{p}/definition`, `PUT /v1/projects/{p}/definition/{section}` (`If-Match`: the section's tag; `412` when it moved) and `POST /v1/projects` (`409` when the name exists). The definition holds no identity: every container commits as the git identity of the box that provisions it and trusts only that box's key, so the `git` and `ssh` blocks and the `${GIT_NAME}`, `${GIT_EMAIL}` and `${SSH_PUBLIC_KEY}` placeholders are gone, and the container's user is always `dev`. Git over SSH is gone too: repos clone over https with the box's forge token, and an ssh-style URL is refused naming its https form. The review pipeline is its own `pipeline` section, out of `agent`. No command takes `-f`/`--file`; `sail migrate` imports nothing from disk; a sync round names each project new to the box with the `sail project apply` that provisions it.
- **A v1 box has no past: install fresh, never migrate.** This release opens only databases it made. `SchemaManager` is one baseline, stamped v1002, identical table for table to what the old chain produced, minus the bookkeeping nothing reads (`data_migrations`, `review_stages.finding_counts`). A database of any other version is refused: one from before v1 names its fix (move it aside, `sail init`), a newer one names `sail upgrade`. The 118-step chain, the on-ramp, the floor and every one-shot data migration are gone, with the importers that read pre-catalog files from disk and the clean-ups that ran only on boxes older than 0.46 (the stray fix agent, pairings of other FDEs, ownerless runs, the unit without live handoff). The sync wire is protocol 7 and has no floor: a peer on another protocol is refused at the hello, an older one told to upgrade itself and a newer one to upgrade main first; a welcome always names the handle and main's file ceiling, and a node no longer reads any field's absence as an older main. `sail migrate` converges this release's box (the database, the demo, the host token and `authorized_keys`, the host drop-ins, the Incus hold, the pty session host) and is still what `sail upgrade` runs with the new binary. Every box in the fleet is reinstalled once for this release.
- **One click brings the project up and opens it.** A project's `processes.<name>` now declares the `port` its app listens on and an `env`, checked when the definition is read: a port is 1024 to 65535 and claimed by one process or service only. `sudo sail project serve <project>` is the one verb that starts a process: it starts the services as apply does, writes each process as a script and a `systemd --user` unit `sail-process-<name>` the dev user runs, its output appended to `~/.sail/processes/<name>.log`, restarts only a process whose command changed and removes one no longer declared, then watches every declared port until it listens or its process exits (`--timeout`, 60 s) and prints `web  3000  listening` or `web  3000  exited 1: <its last line>`; `--stop` stops the processes, `--json` and `--dry-run` as everywhere. `POST /v1/projects/{p}/serve`, `DELETE` of it and `GET /v1/projects/{p}/ports` answer the same, on the API and on the container's socket, for a project the caller may read; bringing a stack up or stopping it is a write, refused to a read-only session. Sail ships a second skill, `sail-portal`, beside `spec-board`: `scripts/portal up|ports|log|stop`, so an agent asked to start the app does what Mast's Portal does. `sail project create` asks for the app when a runtime implies one. An Incus IT that needed the API socket's directory no longer depends on another having made it first.
- **A skill is what the Agent Skills standard says it is, and is shared from wherever it was made.** Sail never read a skill's front matter, so a `SKILL.md` with no `description`, or a `name` other than its folder's, was installed and then skipped by the agent without a word, and its name rule admitted `lint-` and `lint--fix`. `StageSkill` now holds a skill to https://agentskills.io/specification: front matter is required and parsed, `name` must be the folder's by the standard's rule (1 to 64 lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, none first, last or doubled), `description` 1 to 1024 characters, `compatibility` at most 500, `metadata` a map of text to text, `license` and `allowed-tools` text; every other key passes through and the installed `SKILL.md` is the shared one byte for byte; a body over 500 lines is advised against, not refused. A project folder that breaks a rule shows as `invalid: <why>` and is skipped with a warning, with no migration. The container gains `skill ls|show|add|rm` beside `spec`: `skill add <folder>` sends the folder's path over the socket, and the host reads it there as the dev user, its file count and size checked before a byte is read, and shares it exactly as `sail project skills add` does, so a skill made where the work happens reaches every agent and every box. Sharing and removing are a person's: a run's credential is refused with the line a person types instead. `ls`, from either door, lists every skill an agent sees: the project's, sail's, and each one made by hand in an agent's folder, with the agents that read it and the line that shares it. The installer takes over an unstamped copy of a skill whose every file is one of the skill's own, at any depth, as it already did for `spec-board`'s pre-stamp copies. Sharing a folder again now shares exactly what it holds, so a file removed from it stops being shared. `spec` and `skill` find their credential through one generated preamble.
- **An agent in a room reads as working exactly while its turn is open, under one name, and a failed turn reads as failed.** The client-seat rehearsal on 2026-10-26/27 saw Mast's typing dots and "THINKING…" pill stay up with no turn open, one agent named `claude` and `claude-code`, and a signed-out agent's "Not logged in · Please run /login" posted as a reply that ended "· success". A room's event stream now opens with `agent_turn_state`, where its live member's turn stands, so a client believes the box instead of inferring a lost turn's end from message times; a stream the bus drops an event for is ended so its client reconnects and is told again. A turn whose harness flags its result an error is posted in the agent's own words with a synced `error` mark on the message (schema: `room_messages.error`), and its reason is the result's subtype only when that names a failure. Runs and room members carry `agent_name`, the harness's display name ("Claude Code", "Codex CLI"), and `GET /v1/runs?spec=<id>` lists a spec-less room's runs by its id. A client against an older box gets no frame: a draft whose end a dropped stream swallowed stays until its end, its message or the next turn. A box upgraded from a release with no column for a message's mark takes back each question or error mark its journal holds, so a failed turn it received while older reads as failed too. Any event stream that falls far enough behind for the bus to drop one of its events is now ended rather than silently skipping it: `sail events --follow` stops there and says to follow again.
- **A pairing is trusted by the box's containers when it is made, and sail names no editor.** The client-seat rehearsal on 2026-10-26/27 paired a box after `project apply`, as the README orders it, and Mast's key opened the box but not the container until a second apply. `sudo sail fde pair` now writes the key into the SSH user's `authorized_keys` in the container of every project the box runs, running or stopped, beside the keys already there, once the code is printed; a container it cannot write is named after the code with the `sudo sail project apply <it>` that makes it trust the key (a box whose containers cannot be listed is named once, with `sudo sail project apply --all`), and the "only after their next apply" sentence is gone. `--as-workstation-key` takes the key it replaces out of every account of every container of the box in the same step, as unpair does; a container it cannot edit is named with that key's fingerprint and the line to take out by hand. `project apply`, `project connect`, a generated `sail.yaml` and the `${SSH_PUBLIC_KEY}` prompt no longer name Zed; the SSH config snippet stays.

## 0.58.0

- **A command fails for its own reason, says it, and offers a fix the operator can type.** Field rehearsal round 10 found six. `sudo systemctl restart sail-api; sudo sail project apply acme --yes` could fail with "ETag doesn't match": the boot sweep removed and re-added `sail-api-sock` on every start while apply wrote the same instance's config. Every write of a container's Incus config or devices now goes through one door, `ContainerConfig`, which holds the container's lock for that write alone (the device check's read, remove and add as one; a snapshot restore, which puts the config back whole, too, so a sweep meeting a container mid-restore waits for it), so apply and the sweep take turns without the sweep ever waiting on a long provisioning step; the sweep writes nothing when the mount already points at the socket's directory, whose inode no longer moves. `sail agent run --task` cut its branch in `/home/dev/workspace`, which is no repository, and failed with "Failed to prepare the agent session."; it now cuts it as `spec dispatch` does, through the same `WorkBranch`, in each of the project's repos (or, with `--path`, in the deepest repo holding that path as the filesystem resolves it, a linked worktree included; a `--path` that leaves the workspace is refused) and says "Creating branch: <b> in <repo>". A failure that wraps a cause and names no fix prints the cause after its message, through every wrapper that names no fix, with the cause's own fix and never a repeat of the message, and a branch git refuses says what git said. A foreground `agent run` no longer prints its 600-character `incus exec` launch line; `--json` and `--dry-run` keep it. A dispatch refused for a spec that is not pending, or for a branch that already exists, names the command that dispatches it again, `sudo sail spec dispatch -p <p> --spec <id> --restart`, and the API carries the same remedy as data; `--restart` lands on a work branch that already exists whatever the spec's status, so that command works for a pending spec too. A refusal's fix prints indented under its ✗ line.

## 0.57.0

- **A node tells a restored main from a grown one by what sits at its checkpoint.** A main restored in place from an older copy keeps its box id and catalog, and a node told it apart only by its tip falling below the node's checkpoint, so once main had written as many entries as the restore lost the node pulled from its old checkpoint, kept the rows main lost and never read what main wrote right after the restore; round 9's `sudo sail migrate` re-importing one project was enough. A node now keeps, beside each checkpoint, the last entry it read (its seq, id and rev), written before a page adopts anything so a round cut short still names what it took, or that it read none, as a scoped node may; the round's one `Heads` exchange names them, and main's tips name each type whose log no longer holds that entry at that seq, so the node walks every head and withdraws what main lost however far main has grown. An erasure of that entry is still an ordinary round, and so is a compaction of it: compaction keeps the seq, id and rev of each entry it drops, never its content, so a seq compaction emptied is told apart from one a restore filled with other entries, a scoped node is walked when main holds a head of its scope it passed over, and a mark naming an id outside the node's scope is answered as one naming an id main never held. A node and a main on the release before name nothing and sync as before; each node walks main's heads once after the upgrade to keep its first marks. A project now carries its creator, so a node adopting main's revision of a project takes main's `created_by`, and a push still records its pusher, so round 9's leftover `created_by` difference is gone.
- **A flag does what it says, a failure says why, and a row fits the terminal.** Field rehearsal round 9 found five places where sail's output was wrong, silent or too wide. `sail spec dispatch --background` ran the agent in the foreground, because picocli toggles a boolean flag whose default is true: the flag is negatable now, `--background` and no flag both run in the background and `--no-background` runs in this terminal, and a scan of every command fails on any flag true by default without its `--no-` spelling. A foreground dispatch or `agent run` whose agent exits non-zero exits 1 with "✗ The agent exited <code>: <its last line of output>" and "Full log: sudo sail agent log <project>", where it printed the code and exited 0; a background launch is unchanged. A banner's box, table and the branding above them are at most as wide as the terminal stdout reaches, measured once per command and whatever stdin is, a heredoc or `</dev/null` included, or 100 columns into a pipe or a file, counting a wide character or emoji as the two columns it spans by Unicode 17.0's East Asian widths, and a narrow character an emoji presentation selector follows as two, never fewer than a terminal draws; a terminal too narrow for a box's borders gets each line cut at its edge; a value, cell or title too long for its place ends in "…", and a table's widest column gives way first; a tab, line break, cursor move or other control in a value prints as a space and the rest of it as text, only colour and style escapes kept, so a tabbed description or one carrying a cursor move cannot push its row past the terminal; a line outside any box, which carries a command, path or branch to copy, prints whole as one line and the terminal wraps it: `host status` with incus stopped no longer prints 147-column lines, and its JSON keeps the whole error. The Incus row names the installed and candidate builds in one form, both by release or both whole when they share one, with the upgrade command on a row of its own; the JSON adds `incus_installed` and `incus_candidate` is apt's candidate whole. `project apply` and `project demo` on a box whose sail-api never ran refuse before touching incus, naming `sudo sail init --as-main --handle <you>` or `sudo sail init --main <main>`.
- **sail's daemons and the CLI act on the same state, one at a time.** Field rehearsal round 9 found two places where they disagreed. On a fresh main, right after `sudo sail init --as-main`, `sudo sail session ls` and `sudo sail session new` answered "The host could not serve that request.": the pty session host had started before the database moved into `/var/lib/sail` and kept reading `~/.sail`, where it found `no such table: fdes`. `host ssh-identity`, the step of `init --as-main` that moves the database, now writes the `SAIL_DATA_DIR` drop-in for `sail-pty-host` as it does for `sail-api`, and restarts the pty host once the move is done. A node, whose database stays in `~/.sail`, is unchanged. And an apply typed right after `sail-api` restarted, by hand, by `init` or by `upgrade`, failed with "Device “sail-api-sock” doesn't exist", "ETag doesn't match" or "The device already exists": the API server's boot sweep was refreshing the same container's socket device. One process at a time now reconciles a container's sail machinery, under a lock file in the data directory's `locks/`, so the sweep, `project apply`, `project rename`, `sail up`, `migrate` and a dispatch wait for each other on one container while different containers still reconcile in parallel. A shell command that failed without writing to stderr is now reported by its exit code ("Failed to add sail-api-sock to acme: exited 1"), never with an empty reason; the database move's own steps, the drop-in writer and the pty host unit say so the same way. On a main installed before this release, whose pty host has no drop-in, `sudo sail migrate` writes it the bytes sail-api's holds and restarts the pty host onto it, so both daemons read one database everywhere. A `--dry-run` reconcile takes no lock.

## 0.56.0

- **sail installs what it runs on, never the OS, and moves Incus only when asked.** Field rehearsal round 8 found a fresh `sail init --main …` killed at step 2 by the 5-minute deadline on `apt-get install incus` while apt was still downloading; a deadline that lands while dpkg runs leaves the box half-installed. `host init` no longer runs `apt-get upgrade`: step 1 writes the needrestart policy only, and when a package is missing it runs one `apt-get update`, after the Zabbly source is written, then installs only Incus and, for a zfs pool, zfsutils-linux. Every host apt call goes through one seam: no frontend, dpkg keeps changed config files, fetches retry, the dpkg lock is waited on for ten minutes, and nothing is killed on a clock. apt's own status lines show as the step's progress, "waiting for another apt to finish" while the lock is held, and a failure ends on apt's `E:` line, naming the lock's holder when it never came free. The seam runs `-q`, not `-qq`, since `-qq` hides the lock-wait lines and the holder with them. sail now holds the incus packages: `host init` and every `migrate` hold them on a box installed before ("✓ incus held (sudo sail host incus upgrade moves it)", once), so an operator's `apt upgrade` or unattended-upgrades never restarts incusd. `sudo sail host incus upgrade` refreshes apt's index, says when Incus is current, and otherwise lists the live terminal sessions and agent runs the restart ends and asks before it moves the held packages and holds them again. `host status` names an available Incus and the command that moves it, and its JSON gains `incus_candidate`; a same-release rebuild is named whole in both. A candidate older than the installed Incus, a release Zabbly pulled or a build installed by hand, is nothing to move to: `host incus upgrade` says "Incus 6.19 is newer than apt's 6.18. Nothing to move." and `host status` leaves its row alone. `apt-mark hold` goes through the same seam as apt-get, so it too waits on the dpkg lock and runs on no clock, and a move that succeeded but could not be held again says so and names `sudo sail migrate`, which holds it.
- **A failure ends on its own line, with a remedy the command accepts.** Field rehearsal round 8 found five places where a failure printed something other than one readable line and a next step that works. `sudo sail init` no longer prints `host init`'s "re-run with --dry-run" hint (init has no `--dry-run`): a failed step ends setup with "Setup stopped at the step marked ✗ above. Fix the cause above and re-run: sudo sail init …", every word typed kept. `host init` and `project destroy` run on their own still print the hint, and a scan fails on any hint naming an option its command does not take. A provisioning step that fails clears its spinner and prints "[2/8] ✗ Installing Incus (Zabbly stable)", so the reason starts on a line of its own instead of after the last spinner frame (`ProvisionListener.onStepFailed`). `sail events --follow` and `sail agent stream` exit 1 when the API drops or ends the stream without an `error` event, printing "The sail API closed the stream (it restarted or stopped). Follow again: <the command as typed>" in place of "java.io.IOException: closed" or a silent exit 0; an `error` event keeps its refusal. A run's log stream whose tail exited, its container stopped or incus restarted under it, now ends with an `error` event naming that and the command that follows it again, so `agent stream` reports it instead of a restart of the API. A command printed back as typed shows a `--token` or `--git-token` value as `<token>`. `sudo sail project apply -f missing.yaml` names the path and stops, without suggesting the `--file` it was given. `sudo sail host status` with incus stopped keeps its Containers row on one line inside the box; `containers_error` in the JSON keeps incus's whole text.
- **A restored main and its nodes agree, and the README steps leave nothing broken.** Field rehearsal rounds 7 and 8 followed the README's backup and restore and found four gaps. A re-join keeps the node's handle: `sudo sail init --main <main>` and `sudo sail join` without `--handle` join as the handle host.yaml holds, on a terminal and off one, and the `fde add` line names it; another `--handle` is refused before any step, naming the box's handle and `sudo sail host config set sync-handle <h>` (it renamed a `sudo` box to `$SUDO_USER`). ssh's words reach the operator only inside sail's sentence: a sync channel keeps what ssh wrote to stderr for its "ssh said:" and prints none of it, so the re-join probe prints nothing, a key main has not authorised is said once, and a changed host key no longer prints ssh's banner and its own `ssh-keygen -f` advice ahead of sail's remedy. `sudo sail migrate`, and `sail server start`, re-issue the host token when the client config holds one the database does not, as on a main set up again from another box's copy, saying "API token re-issued for this box: the database came from another box."; every other token is kept, and a config token the database holds is left alone (`HostToken.ensure`). After a walk of every head of a main database it synced with before, restored in place or built from a copy on a new box, a node withdraws each spec, room, project or message it heard from main that main no longer offered, its history kept and nothing pushed to rebuild main, and says so once per type ("main no longer holds 2 spec(s) this box had pulled (r-late-1, r-late-2): main was restored from a copy older than this box's last sync. Each stays in its history: sudo sail spec history <id>."); one it changed is refused as "main no longer holds r-late-1: it was restored from an older copy. Yours is in its history: sudo sail spec history r-late-1.", never "may not create for". A former main joining a new main withdraws nothing. A run still under way here, or a project the round keeps whole, is left for a later round, and the type's checkpoint stays at 0 until none is left, so the next round weighs it again; a round cut short still names what it withdrew. `sail server start` reports a client config it cannot read and keeps going.

## 0.55.0

- **A hint names the real next command, and a refusal exits non-zero.** Field rehearsal rounds 6 and 7 found the places where what sail printed was not the next thing to type, or a refusal looked like success. A `[Y/n]` go-ahead nobody answered yes now refuses through `ConsoleHelper.proceed`, as D1's destructive prompts do: with no terminal and no `--yes`, `sudo sail host init` refuses before acting ("Not provisioned: no terminal to confirm on. Re-run with --yes.", exit 1), as do `project apply`'s resume and create prompts, `project init`'s overwrite ("Not written: … Re-run with --output <another path>.") and `project rename` ("Not renamed: … Re-run with --yes."; its `--yes` now skips the prompt, which only `--json` did); declining on a terminal prints "Aborted." and exits 1, the destructive prompts (`project destroy`, `spec delete`, `fde rm`) included, which printed "Aborted."/"Cancelled." and exited 0. Host init's ZFS disk choice refuses the same way and names the disks to pass with `--disk`; with none found it refuses with exit 1; a `--dry-run` asks nothing before rehearsing. A scan fails on any "Aborted." or "Cancelled." printed outside the seam. The "re-run with --dry-run" line follows only a failure sail did not decide, never a refusal it raised on purpose (`CliCommand.Refused`: the no-terminal refusals, `--json` without `--yes`/`--force`, a declined prompt and every "Root privileges required"), nor an argument it rejected, nor one its rules or API answered as a client error (an unsynced roster, an admin-only purge, a write the journal refused). The first sync names an `apply` command per new project ("Provision each with:" then `sudo sail project apply acme`, `sudo sail project apply demo`); a project with no container reads "Project 'demo' has no container on this box. Create it: sudo sail project apply demo" from every command and API answer, `project connect`, `project resources set`, `down` and `agent report` among them, which kept their own copies of the check; `project apply`'s git-token refusal names the project; and a scan fails on a rendered `<name>` (the remaining ones, where the reader picks the value, read `<project>` and `<session>`). Before `sudo sail init`, the no-token hint and `sudo sail sync` name `sudo sail init --as-main --handle <you>` or `sudo sail init --main <main>` instead of `host service install` and `host sync --main`. A scoped node's refusal of an ungranted project, and `project init`'s and `project edit`'s, which now name the box's own handle rather than `<handle>`, reads "If an admin granted it since your last sync, run: sudo sail sync. Otherwise ask an admin to grant it on main: sudo sail fde grant client2 beta." `sail spec comment` prints "✓ Comment posted on <id>." and its message JSON only with `--json`. The installer run as root ends with plain `sail init …`. `sail fde list` on a node shows the scope main last named for the box's own FDE and `-` for every other row, which it showed as `all`; main's is unchanged. A sync main refuses with "Permission denied (publickey)" for the box's own sync key now reads "Main has not authorised this box's key yet. Send its operator this line, then sync again:" and the `fde add` line the join printed, which the join keeps beside the host config (`join.yaml`) and the sync repeats while it still names the box's handle, key, main and pin, then ssh's words. Every value in that line is one shell word (`'Mady M'`, `--key 'ssh-ed25519 …'`), so a name or email holding `$(…)`, a backtick or a quote is passed to `sail fde add` as typed and runs nothing on main.
- **A library update never restarts Incus under live terminals.** On 2026-09-18 and again on 2026-10-22, an unattended Ubuntu security update (perl and libsqlite3, then liblzma5) had needrestart restart `incus.service` in automatic mode, and incusd killed every interactive `incus exec` on shutdown: every container terminal in every project ended, and a Maven run and the agent sessions in a container with it. `sudo sail host init` and every `sail upgrade` (through `migrate`) now write `/etc/needrestart/conf.d/50-sail.conf`, `$nrconf{override_rc}{qr(^incus)} = 0;`, so needrestart lists `incus`, `incus-user` and `incus-lxcfs` as deferred and never restarts them; sail-api and the pty host still restart onto a patched library, as they hand off by design. Init writes it before its `apt-get upgrade`, so a library that upgrade replaces does not restart incusd either; an incus package it upgrades on a re-run still does, as any upgrade of incus does. `sudo sail host update` is gone: it ran `apt-get upgrade -y`, which could upgrade incus from the Zabbly origin or trigger the same restarts, and host packages are the operator's to upgrade with apt (the README says how, with `sudo sail session ls` first). `sail host status` reads the installed Incus version from `incus version` each time it runs, rather than the one `host.yaml` recorded at init, which nothing rewrites after init any more. sail-api's unit carries `SuccessExitStatus=143`, as the pty host's does, so a SIGTERM stop is no longer logged as "Failed with result 'exit-code'". An upgrade reconciles the installed unit with the binary that runs the upgrade, the old one, so a box gets the line from its next upgrade on; `sudo sail host init` writes it at once.
- **A remedy is data until the door that prints it renders it.** Field rehearsal round 6: root logged in directly ran `sail spec create --id r1` twice and was told "edit it with: sudo sail spec update r1", because sail-api, which cannot see who reads its answer, rendered the command for a `sudo` caller. The stores, migrations, sync, authority rules, the harness, the `api` package and the daemons now name a command as a `Remedy` (words, and commands each a verb and its arguments) and never render one; `OperatorCommand` moved to `sail-infra`, so `sail-core` and `sail-harness` cannot reach it, and a scan fails on any use in `api` or a daemon. The CLI's doors render a remedy for the account reading the terminal, so a `sudo` caller reads `sudo sail spec update r1` and a thin client or root logged in directly reads `sail spec update r1`. An API error body carries `remedy` (`[{text}, {run, verb, args}]`) beside an `action` that names each command by its verb alone, and no `sail` sentence. A node's stored sync error keeps its remedy, so `sync status` tells whoever reads it the commands they type (`sudo ssh-keygen -R <main>` through `sudo`, plain for root). Main's session refusal sends its remedy beside the plain reading older nodes read; a pty host's refusal frame carries it after the plain message an older client reads alone; a refused event or log stream, the error event that ends one, and the local lane's `POST /v1/events` refusal answer with the shared error envelope. A sync lane's notices, a daemon's log lines and agent prompts print the plain reading. `OperatorCommand.elsewhere()` and `unknownReader()` are gone. A root-only refusal names the command as typed: "Root privileges required" re-ran a shorter command than the caller's: `project resources set gamma --cpu 4` dropped `--cpu`, so the re-run was refused; `project destroy --purge --yes` dropped both, so it destroyed locally instead of purging everywhere; `host sync` and `join` dropped `--handle/--name/--email`; `init` dropped every flag. Every root-only verb now names the caller's own words, shell-quoted, with `sudo` in front, through one helper (`RootRequired`), `Main`'s refusal of a non-owner included; `host keys sync` and `host ssh-identity` name theirs too. `host sync` refuses a non-root caller before it reads the host config, so a fresh box says root is needed rather than that it is not initialized. One file is the box's workstation key: a `sudo` caller who owns a box in their home had two: `${SSH_PUBLIC_KEY}` read theirs and a pairing, `host config set ssh-public-key` and step 6 read root's, so a container could be handed two keys. `SailPaths.workstationPublicKeyPath()` resolves the one file, in the box owner's home, for every reader and writer. `host config set ssh-public-key` writes it under `PairingTrust`, so an apply waits for the change as it waits for a pairing.

## 0.54.0

- **A node never says "in sync" with a main that isn't the one it synced with.** Field rehearsal round 4 deleted main's VM and set it up again empty at the same IP; the node re-pinned, main ran the `fde add` line, and `sudo sail sync` printed "✓ Already in sync with main." while the new main held only `demo` and the node `gamma`, so a spec the node then filed under `gamma` was pushed into a main that holds no such project. Every database now carries a catalog id (`DatabaseCatalog`), random, minted with the database (an existing one at its upgrade) and kept by every upgrade and every copy of the file, and main's welcome names it. A node records the catalog of the first main database that welcomes it (a node from before this release on its next round) and refuses any other before anything is pushed, adopted or recorded, with exit 1: "Main at <target> is not the database this box synced with (catalog <old>, now <new>). Restore main's database (/var/lib/sail/sail.db) from a copy, then sync again." A main older than this release names no catalog and could be any database, so it is refused too, with exit 1 and before anything moves: "Main at <target> names no database catalog, so this box cannot tell whether it is the database it synced with: upgrade main ('sudo sail upgrade' on main), then sync again." **Fleet note:** upgrade main before its nodes; an upgraded node does not sync with a main on an older release. It is decided on the catalog, not the box id, so a new database under the old `host.yaml` is refused too instead of silently skipped below the node's old checkpoint. Main set up again from a copy of its database is the same main: its nodes push what they wrote since the copy, and "Already in sync with main." follows. The README and ARCHITECTURE.md say to back up `/var/lib/sail/sail.db` and how to restore it; a copy taken before this release is minted a new catalog when restored, so take one after upgrading. Every join now prints the key it pinned, "Pinned main's host key SHA256:…. Check it on main: sudo ssh-keygen -lf /etc/ssh/ssh_host_ed25519_key.pub", including a re-join after a host-key rotation that then says "Main already has this box's key"; the changed-key recovery reads "If main was rebuilt from a copy of its database, remove the old key with '…', re-run '…' and compare the fingerprint it prints with main's (…); otherwise something between this box and main is answering for it."; and the re-join's probe of main prints nothing of its own (the stray "⚠ hello: Sync channel closed before main replied." line is gone). A main restored in place from an older copy of its database keeps its box id, so its log sits below the node's checkpoint; the node then walks every head again from the copy instead of paging nothing and saying it is in sync. A box that stops being a node forgets main's catalog as it forgets its scope.
- **Rows written together order as minted on a clock of any resolution.** Cutting v0.53.0, the macOS release job failed `ReviewLoopEveryStateTest` once, as v0.48.0 did. An instant is stored as its text, which drops trailing zeros, so `…51.123Z` sorts after the later `…51.123400Z` and `…51Z` after `…51.001Z`; a microsecond clock lands on such a value one time in a thousand. The stores ordered runs and reviews by that text, so the review loop could read a fix run as newer than the reviewer launched after it and ignore that reviewer's stop. Runs and reviews now order by their UUIDv7 id, as each box minted them; stored instants are unchanged and nothing migrates. The review loop fixture's fixed-width clock is gone.
- **A box that stops being a node keeps nothing of the node it was.** The delta review of `sail-sync-is-exact` found two ways a box still carried node state after it stopped being one. Re-running `sudo sail host init` rewrote `host.yaml` from scratch, dropping the sync role, main, handle, box id and passkey settings, so a node or main turned standalone and orphaned every checkpoint its peers held for it; a re-run now refreshes only the fields it provisions and keeps the rest of `host.yaml`, read again under the box's round lock just before it writes, so a `host sync`, `join` or handle change that finished while the packages installed is kept, not undone.
